  <div  class="popup-content">
    
<form class="search-form" role="search" method="get" id="" action="https://www.group-ib.com/">
  <input type="search" placeholder="Search" name="s" id="search-input" value=""/>
  <button type="submit" class="search-button"><img src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/search-icon.svg" alt="Search Icon"></button>

<style>
  .hidden {
    display: none;
  }
</style>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
  const openSearchPopupBtns = [...document.querySelectorAll('.open-search-popup')];
  const closeSearchPopupBtns = [...document.querySelectorAll('.close-search-popup')];
  const searchPopup = document.querySelector('.search-popup');

  const closePopup = () => {
    searchPopup.style.display = 'none';
    openSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.remove('hidden'));
    closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.add('hidden'));
  };

  openSearchPopupBtns.forEach(openSearchPopupBtn => {
    openSearchPopupBtn.addEventListener('click', (e) => {
      e.preventDefault();

      searchPopup.style.display = 'block';
      openSearchPopupBtn.classList.add('hidden');
      closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.remove('hidden'));
    });
  });

  closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(closeSearchPopupBtn => {
    closeSearchPopupBtn.addEventListener('click', () => {
      closePopup();
    });
  });

  document.addEventListener('click', (event) => {
    if (!searchPopup.contains(event.target) && !openSearchPopupBtns.some(btn => btn.contains(event.target)) && !closeSearchPopupBtns.some(btn => btn.contains(event.target))) {
      setTimeout(() => {
        if (!searchPopup.contains(event.target)) {
          closePopup();
        }
      }, 100);
    }
  });
</script>    </div>

                            <h1 class="header__title title-h2 mb-24 mt-md-48 mt-lg-64 mt-xl-64">BlackCat Ransomware: Tactics, Techniques & Mitigation Strategies</h1><p class="header__text mb-xs-48 mb-64 mb-lg-96 mb-xl-96">An analysis of the BlackCat ransomware affiliate program</p><div class="post-info__date mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48"><div class="body2">May 14, 2021 · <span id="reading-time"></span> min to read · Ransomware</div></div><div class="post-info__image mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48"><picture><source srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/img-1440-19-min.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img data-no-lazy="" fetchpriority="high" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/img-1440-19-min.jpg" alt="" class="webpexpress-processed"></picture></div><div class="post-info__tags"><div class="post-info__tag body2">ALPHV</div><div class="post-info__tag body2">BlackCat</div><div class="post-info__tag body2">Ransomware</div></div>                        </div>
                    </div>
                </div>
                <div class="blog-post-content" id="blog-post-content">
                    <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>In February 2024, Change Healthcare, a subsidiary of UnitedHealth Group, faced every company&#8217;s worst nightmare. Attackers struck swiftly, encrypted vital patient data, and demanded an eye-watering ransom of $22 million. Even after paying the ransom, <a href="https://www.ibm.com/think/news/change-healthcare-22-million-ransomware-payment">Change Healthcare did not get its data back</a>.</p>
<p>Behind this attack was BlackCat, also known as ALPHV, a ransomware group notorious for its ruthless efficiency, cunning affiliate tactics, and aggressive pursuit of high-value targets. Despite global crackdowns and arrests, ransomware poses the greatest cybersecurity threat in 2025. Underground forums continue to thrive, selling “access” and hiring “penetration testers”.</p>
<p>In this article, you&#8217;ll learn the latest intelligence on BlackCat ransomware &#8211; its evolution, extortion methods, notable attacks across industries, and the defensive strategies your organization can adopt today to detect, prevent, and respond to ransomware threats.</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="gallery swiper gallery-swiper-423"><div class="swiper-wrapper"><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-1.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Images used by ALPHV in the beginning" title="1-min (1)" class="webpexpress-processed" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-1.jpg"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-1.jpg" alt="Images used by ALPHV in the beginning" title="1-min (1)" class="webpexpress-processed"></noscript></picture><p class="small wp-caption-text">Images used by ALPHV in the beginning</p></div><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-2-1.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Images used by ALPHV Updated logo" title="2-min (2)" class="webpexpress-processed" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-2-1.png"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-2-1.png" alt="Images used by ALPHV Updated logo" title="2-min (2)" class="webpexpress-processed"></noscript></picture><p class="small wp-caption-text">Updated logo</p></div></div><div class="swiper-pagination"></div><div class="swiper-button-next"></div><div class="swiper-button-prev"></div></div></div>

                  <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><h3>Key Discoveries:</h3>
<ul>
<li aria-level="1">BlackCat (ALPHV) surfaced in late 2021 and quickly became the second most prolific global ransomware strain, responsible for hundreds of millions in extorted payments.</li>
<li aria-level="1">BlackCat operates as a Ransomware-as-a-Service (RaaS) with affiliates adopting whatever intrusion techniques yield results, including social engineering and malvertising.</li>
<li aria-level="1">In just a few years, BlackCat’s operators and affiliates have hit organizations from healthcare and hospitality to critical infrastructure, leaving a trail of encrypted systems and stolen data in their wake.</li>
<li aria-level="1">BlackCat affiliates routinely engage in double extortion, stealing sensitive information and threatening to publish it if the ransom isn’t paid. Some affiliates have even layered in DDoS attack threats to coerce payment​.</li>
<li aria-level="1">In 2024, the group grabbed headlines with one of the largest data breaches in history and even appeared to “shut down” amid internal turmoil, only to reemerge under a new guise.</li>
<li aria-level="1">A similar Rust-based ransomware called “Cicada3301” appeared six months after, which cybersecurity analysts suspect is BlackCat 2.0 operating under a new name.</li>
</ul>
<p>Advanced threat intelligence and detection solutions are helping organizations to identify and block BlackCat operations early. In an incident, strong offline backups and an incident response plan can reduce BlackCat’s impact</p>
<h2>What is BlackCat (ALPHV) Ransomware?</h2>
<p>BlackCat operates as a <a href="/resources/knowledge-hub/raas/">Ransomware-as-a-Service (RaaS) </a>model and is considered one of the most sophisticated RaaS operations. The Russian ransomware group emerged shortly after the <a href="/blog/blackmatter-ransomware/">BlackMatter</a>/DarkSide shut down in 2021, suggesting a possible regrouping of veteran hackers under the ALPHV brand​.</p>
<p><b>Security researchers unofficially </b><b>called </b><b>it BlackCat </b>for using two logos: a black cat and a knife dripping with blood. ALPHV members later attempted to move away from romanticizing crime by changing the design of their logo, but the name BlackCat has stuck.</p>
<p>BlackCat started its activity in December 2021, when a campaign to attract new affiliates was advertised on underground forums. Not only was it led by experienced operators, but BlackCat’s malware was written in the Rust programming language – one of the first significant ransomware families. Rust’s efficiency and cross-platform capabilities enabled the developers to add custom features and hinder reverse engineering.</p>
<p>&nbsp;</p>
<div id="attachment_23583" style="width: 817px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23583" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23583" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20807%20416'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Description of the affiliate program on an underground forum" width="807" height="416" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/3-min-1-1.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23583" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23583" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/3-min-1-1.png" alt="Description of the affiliate program on an underground forum" width="807" height="416" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-23583" class="wp-caption-text">Description of the affiliate program on an underground forum</p></div>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="accordionList21"><div class="accordion-item"><div class="accordion-item__title"><div class="title-h5">Translation</div><div class="accordion-item__icon"><span class="material-icons">arrow_drop_down</span></div></div><div class="accordion-item__text"><p><strong>INTRO</strong></p>
<p>Welcome to our affiliate program.<br />
We’ve taken into account all the advantages and weaknesses of previous affiliate programs and are proud to present to you ALPHV, a new generation of ransomware.<br />
All the software has been developed from scratch, with decentralization of all web resources ensured architecturally. A unique onion domain is generated for every new campaign. Every affiliate has access through a unique onion domain (hello lockbit).<br />
A proprietary data center for storing file leaks bigger than 100 TB.<br />
Top recovery companies, which have worked with darkside, revil, etc. are already collaborating with us.<br />
Chat support is available 24/7, but you can negotiate yourself if you’d like.</p>
<p><strong>SECURITY</strong></p>
<p>We are fully prepared for present-day conditions, complying with all infrastructure and affiliate security requirements. Our affiliate program architecturally rules out any connections with forums(hello revil), has algorithms for data self-deletion after a certain time, and has an integrated mixer with an actual break in the chain(not to be confused with Wasabi, BitMix, and others), as you get perfectly clean coins from foreign exchanges. Our backend does not know the wallets your coins are sent to. The infrastructure is divided into “nodes”, which are interconnected via an entire network of intermediaries within the onion network and are located behind NAT+FW.<br />
Even after receiving a full-on cmdshell, the attacker cannot reveal the real IP address of the server (hello conti)</p>
<p><strong>ACCOUNT</strong></p>
<p>If your account has not been active for two weeks, it will be locked, then deleted. Inorder to avoid that, we recommend notifying the admins about vacations, breaks, etc.</p>
<p>Rates are dynamic and depend on the size of a single payment for each company, namely:</p>
<ul>
<li>Up to $1.5M &#8211; 80%</li>
<li>Up to $3.0M &#8211; 85%</li>
<li>$3M and more &#8211; 90%</li>
</ul>
</div></div>
                      <script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
                        jQuery(window).on('load', function () {
                          let accordion21 = document.querySelector('.accordionList21');
                          if (accordion21) {
                            let items = accordion21.querySelectorAll('.accordion-item');
                            let title = accordion21.querySelectorAll('.accordion-item__title');

                            function toggleAccordion() {

                              items.forEach((item) => {
                                if (thisItem == item) {
                                  thisItem.classList.toggle('active');
                                  return;
                                }
                                item.classList.remove('active');
                              });
                            }

                            title.forEach((question) => question.addEventListener('click', toggleAccordion));
                          } 
                        });
                      </script>
                      </div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>In this campaign, potential affiliates were offered a brand new kind of ransomware family developed “from scratch” in the Rust programming language, which is a popular cross-platform programming language for creating secure and effective applications.  The use of Rust to create ransomware was a major event in the world of cybercrime.</p>
<div id="attachment_23584" style="width: 818px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23584" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23584" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20808%20559'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Description of the BlackCat ransomware family" width="808" height="559" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/4-min-1-1.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23584" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23584" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/4-min-1-1.png" alt="Description of the BlackCat ransomware family" width="808" height="559" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-23584" class="wp-caption-text">Description of the BlackCat ransomware family</p></div>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="accordionList625"><div class="accordion-item"><div class="accordion-item__title"><div class="title-h5">Translation</div><div class="accordion-item__icon"><span class="material-icons">arrow_drop_down</span></div></div><div class="accordion-item__text"><p>SOFTWARE</p>
<p>The software has been developed from scratch without using any templates or leaked source codes of other ransomware. You can choose between:</p>
<p>4 encryption modes:</p>
<ul>
<li>Full &#8211; full file encryption. The most secure and the slowest.</li>
<li>Fast &#8211; encryption of the first N megabytes. Not recommended, the least secure option, but the fastest.</li>
<li>DotPattern &#8211; encryption of N megabytes with an M interval. May function worse than Fast in terms of both speed and encryption strength if configured incorrectly.</li>
<li>Auto. Depending on the file type and size, the locker(both in windows and *nix / esxi) chooses the most optimal(in terms of the speed / security ratio) strategy for processing files.</li>
<li>SmartPattern &#8211; encryption of N megabytes with a percentage interval. By default, it encrypts with 10-megabyte blocks with an interval of 10% of the file starting with the header. The most optimal mode in terms of the speed\encryption strength ratio.</li>
</ul>
<p>2 encryption algorithms:</p>
<ul>
<li>ChaCha20</li>
<li>AES</li>
</ul>
<p>In auto mode, the software determines the presence of hardware support for AES(present in all modern processors) and uses it. If there is no AES support, the software encrypts files using ChaCha20.</p>
<p>The software is cross-platform, i.e., if you mount Windows disks in Linux or vice versa, the decryptor will be able to decrypt files.</p>
<p>Supported OSs</p>
<ul>
<li>The entire Windows line from Windows 7 and above (we tested it on 7, 8.1, 10, 11, 2008r2, 2012, 2016, 2019, 2022 ); XP and 2003 can be encrypted through SMB.</li>
<li>ESXI (tested on 5.5, 6.5, 7.0.2u)</li>
<li>Debian (tested on 7, 8, 9)</li>
<li>Ubuntu (tested on 18.04, 20.04)</li>
<li>ReadyNAS, Synology</li>
</ul>
</div></div>
                      <script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">

                      </div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>The new RaaS program took into account the hostile experience of their predecessors, namely the DarkSide, BlackMatter, and REvil affiliate programs. After their notorious attacks against major companies, these groups came under the spotlight of security researchers and law enforcers, who, together with samples, obtained access to victims’ pages containing correspondence with threat actors, where they often interfered.</p>
<div id="attachment_23585" style="width: 820px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23585" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23585" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20810%20181'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Information about using access tokens" width="810" height="181" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min-1-1.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23585" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23585" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min-1-1.png" alt="Information about using access tokens" width="810" height="181" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-23585" class="wp-caption-text">Information about using access tokens</p></div>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="accordionList36"><div class="accordion-item"><div class="accordion-item__title"><div class="title-h5">Translation</div><div class="accordion-item__icon"><span class="material-icons">arrow_drop_down</span></div></div><div class="accordion-item__text"><p>Since binaries have been leaking to analysts lately, and VT premium lets you download samples and get readmes, random people may appear in chats and disrupt negotiations (hello darkside), when launching the software you MUST use the flag &#8211;access-token. cmdline arguments are not passed to AVs, which will ensure that your correspondence with the victim is confidential. For the same reason, every encrypted computer generates a unique ID used for dividing chats.</p>
<p>There is a feature for uploading files from the MEGA service, you provide a link to files and they are automatically uploaded to your servers.</p>
<p>The complete description of all features can be found in the FAQ section.</p>
</div></div>
                      <script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
                        jQuery(window).on('load', function () {
                          let accordion36 = document.querySelector('.accordionList36');
                          if (accordion36) {

                          } 
                        });
                      </script>
                      </div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>To avoid previous mistakes, the BlackCat authors fitted ransomware with a mandatory command line parameter containing an access token provided by the RaaS owners to their affiliates and their ransomware suite.</p>
<p>The ransomware uses the access token to determine the access key, which is added to a Tor link for the victim to access their page.</p>
<div id="attachment_23586" style="width: 991px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23586" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23586" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20981%2083'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Contents of the help text file provided to RaaS affiliates" width="981" height="83" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-4.webp" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23586" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23586" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-4.webp" alt="Contents of the help text file provided to RaaS affiliates" width="981" height="83" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-23586" class="wp-caption-text">Contents of the help text file provided to RaaS affiliates</p></div>
<p>This link is saved in the ransom note, which is created as a text file in each catalog with encrypted files.</p>
<div id="attachment_23587" style="width: 866px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23587" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23587" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20856%20571'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Ransom note" width="856" height="571" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/7-4.webp" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23587" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23587" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/7-4.webp" alt="Ransom note" width="856" height="571" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-23587" class="wp-caption-text">Ransom note</p></div>
<p>When victims published information from their chats, BlackCat affiliates would punish them by increasing the ransom demands. At the start of their activity, the threat actors were even more radical; for instance, they deleted the victim’s encryption keys to intimidate future victims.</p>
<div id="attachment_23572" style="width: 1047px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23572" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23572" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201037%20256'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Information about deleting the encryption keys of a victim published by BlackCat operators" width="1037" height="256" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/8-3.webp" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23572" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23572" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/8-3.webp" alt="Information about deleting the encryption keys of a victim published by BlackCat operators" width="1037" height="256" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-23572" class="wp-caption-text">Information about deleting the encryption keys of a victim published by BlackCat operators</p></div>
<div id="attachment_23573" style="width: 812px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23573" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23573" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20802%20913'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Victim’s personal page screenshot" width="802" height="913" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/9-1.webp" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23573" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23573" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/9-1.webp" alt="Victim’s personal page screenshot" width="802" height="913" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-23573" class="wp-caption-text">Victim’s personal page</p></div>
<h2>BlackCat Extortion Methods</h2>
<h3>Double/Triple Extortion and Leak Sites</h3>
<p>A hallmark of BlackCat’s operations is the use of double extortion and triple extortion techniques:</p>
<ul>
<li aria-level="1">The stolen information is published on BlackCat’s Dedicated Leak Site (DLS).</li>
<li aria-level="1">To mount pressure on the victim, BlackCat affiliates may threaten to send sensitive data to the victim’s competitors, partners, customers, mass media, law enforcement, etc.</li>
<li aria-level="1">BlackCat ransomware victims may receive threats of a DDoS attack launched against their infrastructure to extort higher payments, on top of data leaks​.</li>
<li aria-level="1">The gang prefers cryptocurrency (BTC or Monero) for payment​ , and negotiations are conducted via an encrypted chat hosted on a unique onion domain assigned to each victim.</li>
</ul>
<p>U.S. authorities report that <a href="https://www.justice.gov/archives/opa/pr/justice-department-disrupts-prolific-alphvblackcat-ransomware-variant">BlackCat targeted over 1,000 organizations</a> worldwide in its first 18 months of activity​. In 2022, <b>BlackCat launched “ALPHV Collections,</b>” a searchable leak platform on the open web (with a clearnet domain) that indexed victim data for anyone to browse​. BlackCat’s leaked data was indexed on the open web, making it easily accessible to the public and search engines.</p>
<p>In some cases, the gang went further by leaking files on public websites, impersonating the victim’s own domain (using lookalike/typosquatting URLs), a tactic intended to draw even more attention to the breach​. BlackCat’s leak site entries provide proof documents and a short, threatening description of the breach, openly shaming the victim.</p>
<p>A <a href="https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/cybersecurity-advisories/aa23-353a">joint FBI/CISA alert</a> noted that from December 2023 to February 2024, BlackCat’s leak site listed nearly 70 new victims, with the healthcare sector the most commonly victimized. Additionally, our investigators have found that BlackCat operators increasingly allude to auctioning stolen data or selling it to competitors if the victim balks.</p>
<p>As published in Group-IB’s <a href="/resources/research-hub/ransomware-readiness/">Ransomware Readiness white paper</a>, BlackCat was responsible for <b>427 known ransomware attacks in 2023</b>, where victim data was posted on leak sites, making it the second most active group that year (behind only LockBit’s 1,079 incidents).</p>
<p>BlackCat had also unveiled a public data leak API on its site, allowing anyone to fetch updates about new victims and leaked files automatically​. This helped to amplify the visibility of BlackCat’s leaks when ransom negotiations faltered (e.g., after a high-profile victim like <a href="https://www.bitdefender.com/en-gb/blog/hotforsecurity/estee-lauder-internal-data-stolen-after-being-hit-by-two-separate-ransomware-attacks">Estée Lauder </a>refused to pay).</p>
<p>The <a href="https://www.justice.gov/archives/opa/pr/justice-department-disrupts-prolific-alphvblackcat-ransomware-variant">FBI’s December 2023 operation</a>, notably, provided many victims with a free decryptor, undermining BlackCat’s leverage​. In response, BlackCat’s admin raged on their blog and encouraged hitting more healthcare targets (viewed as more likely to pay quickly)​.</p>
<div id="attachment_23574" style="width: 755px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23574" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23574" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20745%20441'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Chat with a victim screenshot" width="745" height="441" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-1.webp" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23574" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23574" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-1.webp" alt="Chat with a victim screenshot" width="745" height="441" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-23574" class="wp-caption-text">Chat with a victim</p></div>
<p>At the time of writing, the stolen documents of 93 affected companies that refused to pay a ransom were published on BlackCat’s DLS. <strong>We estimate that the overall number of BlackCat victims since December 2021 is about 140.</strong></p>
<p>&nbsp;</p>
<p><img decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23575" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201280%201100'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="BlackCat's DLS" width="1280" height="1100" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/11-3.webp" /><noscript><img decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23575" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/11-3.webp" alt="BlackCat's DLS" width="1280" height="1100" /></noscript></p>
<p>In some cases, BlackCat affiliates have flipped the script entirely by forgoing encryption. For example, in February 2023, BlackCat claimed to have breached <a href="https://www.theverge.com/2023/6/19/23765895/reddit-hack-phishing-leak-api-pricing-steve-huffman">Reddit</a> and stolen around 80 GB of data without deploying ransomware. They demanded $4.5 million and an end to Reddit’s planned API pricing changes as “ransom,” threatening to leak the stolen internal data​.</p>
<p>This “data kidnapping” approach shows that if encrypting a target’s systems is too difficult or not the goal, BlackCat will simply steal valuable data and use the fear of a leak as leverage, even if no ransomware is deployed.</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="gallery swiper gallery-swiper-580"><div class="swiper-wrapper"><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="distribution of blackcat victims by country" title="12" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/12-3.webp"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/12-3.webp" alt="distribution of blackcat victims by country" title="12"></noscript><p class="small wp-caption-text"></p></div><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="distribution of blackcat victims by industry" title="13" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/13-2.webp"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/13-2.webp" alt="distribution of blackcat victims by industry" title="13"></noscript><p class="small wp-caption-text"></p></div></div><div class="swiper-pagination"></div><div class="swiper-button-next"></div><div class="swiper-button-prev"></div></div></div>

                  <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><h2>BlackCat Ransomware Affiliates &#8211; Tactics, Techniques and Procedures</h2>
<p>BlackCat affiliates were behind some of the most brazen social engineering attacks of 2023, including the high-profile breach of MGM Resorts. In another significant case, Caesars Entertainment was targeted by Scattered Spider, a group closely associated with ALPHV.</p>
<h3>1. Gaining Access to the Network</h3>
<p>Since a single affiliate program may involve different threat actors, techniques used for obtaining initial access may differ. Further, affiliates may use the services of initial access brokers, who sell access to companies’ compromised infrastructures.</p>
<p>As part of investigating security incidents, we have seen the following techniques:</p>
<ol>
<li><strong>Exploiting public-facing applications.</strong><br />
This technique gained popularity in 2021 among both affiliates and initial access brokers due to a lot of vulnerabilities being discovered that allowed arbitrary code execution in a variety of applications. In the case of BlackCat, the attackers exploited a set of vulnerabilities known as ProxyShell (CVE-2021-34473, CVE-2021-34523, CVE-2021-31207), which enabled them to place a <a href="https://www.group-ib.com/resources/knowledge-hub/web-shell/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">web shell</a> on a vulnerable Microsoft Exchange server and then conduct post-exploitation activities.</li>
<li><strong>Using remote access tools.</strong><br />
Access via publicly accessible terminal servers remains the most popular technique for gaining initial access and BlackCat affiliates used it in some cases. In addition to terminal servers, access to the target infrastructure could be gained via a VPN; many organizations still do not use multifactor authentication, which enables ransomware operators to easily use accounts whose data have been stolen using stealers, for example.</li>
<li><b>Malvertising and cloning legitimate websites</b>Affiliates set up fake websites (or hijack real ones) to host trojanized software or credential stealers. They then run malicious ads or search engine poisoning to draw in victims looking for popular software cracks or tools.When an unwitting user downloads the payload, it installs a backdoor or keylogger that the attackers later use to pivot into the corporate network. This technique allows BlackCat to compromise companies with poor web filtering and might not fall for direct phishing.</li>
</ol>
<h3>2. Establishing Foothold</h3>
<p>Having obtained initial access, the attackers copy a set of tools (in full or in part) to the compromised host and seek to ensure persistence and gain access to privileged accounts to be able to move across the network.</p>
<ol>
<li aria-level="1">For additional capabilities to access the compromised network, the attackers could use tunnels built using ngrok or gost, or software such as TeamViewer or ScreenConnect.</li>
<li aria-level="1">Attackers also install backdoors or C2 beacons for persistent access. For example, they deploy a <b>Cobalt Strike Beacon</b> or CSharp-Streamer RAT.</li>
<li aria-level="1">In most incidents, <b>BlackCat affiliates relied on legitimate tools</b> to extract authentication data by dumping the <b>LSASS</b> (Local Security Authority Server Service) process. For instance, the threat actors used ProcDump and exploited the <b>MiniDump </b>feature of the legitimate library <i>comsvcs.dll.</i></li>
<li aria-level="1">Some attackers went beyond LSASS and used various <b>NirSoft tools</b> to extract authentication data from the registry, web browsers, and other storage spaces.</li>
</ol>
<h3>3. Network Discovery</h3>
<p>Having established a beachhead, BlackCat affiliates map out the victim’s network. They need to identify what machines are present, how they’re connected, and which ones hold vital data or critical services.</p>
<p>Attackers often start with classic network scanning commands and discovery tools:</p>
<ol>
<li aria-level="1"><b>SoftPerfect Network Scanner scans the network</b>, a popular tool among ransomware groups.</li>
<li aria-level="1"><b>ADRecon</b> collects information about Active Directory, another standard tool among REvil and BlackMatter affiliates.</li>
<li aria-level="1">To collect data about available local and network drives, the<b> NS tool </b>is especially popular with affiliates that use terminal servers to gain initial access.</li>
</ol>
<h3>Key Assets Discovery, Network Propagation and Data Exfiltration</h3>
<p>With enough privileges in the target IT infrastructure, the attackers start moving to key nodes, which will enable them to download the most important information and do away with backups.</p>
<ol>
<li aria-level="1">BlackCat affiliates use <b>legitimate techniques </b>(such as <b>RDP</b>) and noisier ones (e.g., <b>Impacket:</b> wmiexec and smbexec in particular; and Cobalt Strike) to move across the network.</li>
<li aria-level="1"><b>PuTTY</b> is often used to gain access to the part of the infrastructure running on Linux.</li>
<li aria-level="1">Before being exfiltrated, <b>data is archived using 7-Zip and</b> <b>uploaded to the MEGA file </b>sharing service <b>using the Rclone utility</b>.</li>
<li aria-level="1">In some cases, <b>affiliates used ExMatter,</b> an exfiltration tool that was seen earlier in the arsenal of <b>BlackMatter affiliates.</b><b> </b></li>
</ol>
<h3>Deployment Preparation</h3>
<p>BlackCat ransomware deployment is preceded by the erasure or encryption of available backup copies and collection of additional credentials that would allow the attackers to infect the Linux segment, in addition to Windows.</p>
<p>Despite the detectability of BlackCat samples not being high, some affiliates seek to disable antivirus software before moving on to the deployment stage.</p>
<h3>BlackCat Ransomware Deployment</h3>
<p>The propagation of BlackCat in the victim’s IT infrastructure is achieved by either modifying group policies (which results in a scheduled task being created, on each host, that launches the malicious file) or using PsExec.</p>
<p>The ransomware is written in Rust. Many researchers rightfully consider BlackCat as one of the most sophisticated ransomware groups out there at the moment. BlackCat programs are feature-rich and offer flexible custom settings due to the use of various configuration data and command line arguments.</p>
<p>There are BlackCat versions for Windows (32bit) and Linux (32bit and 64bit). The 64bit Linux version primarily targets ESXi servers. In March 2022, a new version of BlackCat emerged, called ALPHV MORPH. On underground forums its authors proudly claimed that thanks to obfuscation, antivirus software is practically unable to detect it.</p>
<div id="attachment_23578" style="width: 706px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23578" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23578" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20696%20236'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Description of ALPHV MORPHV features on an underground forum" width="696" height="236" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/14-min-2.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23578" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23578" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/14-min-2.png" alt="Description of ALPHV MORPHV features on an underground forum" width="696" height="236" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-23578" class="wp-caption-text">Description of ALPHV MORPHV features on an underground forum</p></div>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="accordionList639"><div class="accordion-item"><div class="accordion-item__title"><div class="title-h5">Translation</div><div class="accordion-item__icon"><span class="material-icons">arrow_drop_down</span></div></div><div class="accordion-item__text"><p><strong>LOCKER</strong></p>
<p>1. We are proud to present ALPHV MORPH. Without going into the spicy details, we inform that the binary is completely cleared every hour. In addition to recrypting calls, stings, and other things, the RUST compiler makes it possible to enrich every build with unique runtime junk, which in the end yielded fantastic results. At the moment, no AV detects it(not to be confused with edr! we did not test it on sentinel), including defender with the cloud disabled – the binary is not deleted even after the machine is fully encrypted. In test mode so far, intentionally(!), it is available to everyone via Build-&gt;Obfuscated. In the future, this functionality will only be available to affiliates with the + status.<br />
2. Minor fixes in the locker’s operation.</p>
<p>p.s. There is no AV for ESXI yet, but we already have a linux polymorph 🙂 That’s right, linux is also morphed every hour just because we can.</p>
</div></div>
                      <script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
                        jQuery(window).on('load', function () {
                          let accordion639 = document.querySelector('.accordionList639');
                          if (accordion639) {
                            let items = accordion639.querySelectorAll('.accordion-item');
                            let title = accordion639.querySelectorAll('.accordion-item__title');

                            function toggleAccordion() {
                              let thisItem = this.parentNode;
                              items.forEach((item) => {
                                if (thisItem == item) {
                                  thisItem.classList.toggle('active');
                                  return;
                                }
                                item.classList.remove('active');
                              });
                            }

                            title.forEach((question) => question.addEventListener('click', toggleAccordion));
                          } 
                        });

                      </div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>It has been mentioned before that launching BlackCat ransomware requires specifying the value of an access token in the command line parameter — <strong>access-token</strong>.</p>
<ul>
<li aria-level="1">In earlier versions, whether the token value is correct was not checked, while the access key is calculated using the entered token value; the program will be launched, and files will be encrypted, but accessing the victim’s panel would be impossible.</li>
<li aria-level="1">In the ALPHV MORPH version, the first 16 characters of the access token are used as a key to decrypt configuration data, which is why, if incorrect data is entered, the ransomware will not start.</li>
<li aria-level="1">To bypass User Account Control (UAC), BlackCat escalates privileges using the ICMLuaUtil COM interface. Privileges can also be escalated using the Masquerade PEB method.</li>
<li aria-level="1">BlackCat ransomware may attempt to authenticate using stolen credentials contained in configuration data.</li>
</ul>
<p>When launched, <strong>BlackCat allows symbolic links from a deleted item to local and remote items</strong>:</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="code"><div class="code__wrapper">fsutil behavior set SymlinkEvaluation R2L:1

fsutil behavior set SymlinkEvaluation R2R:1</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>Stops IIS by executing the following command:</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="code"><div class="code__wrapper">iisreset.exe /stop</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>Deletes volume shadow copies:</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="code"><div class="code__wrapper">vssadmin.exe Delete Shadows /all /quiet

wmic.exe Shadowcopy Delete</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>Disables recovery in Windows boot menu:</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="code"><div class="code__wrapper">bcdedit /set {default}

bcdedit /set {default} recoveryenabled No</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>Clears Windows event logs:</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="code"><div class="code__wrapper">for /F "tokens=*" %1 in ('wevtutil.exe el') DO wevtutil.exe cl "%1"</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>In addition, the ransomware ends processes and stops services specified in the configuration.</p>
</div>
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>It should be noted that <strong>BlackCat for Windows can independently propagate itself</strong> in the local area network as a network worm. To do so, the legitimate PsExec utility contained in the body of the ransomware is used together with stolen credentials specified in the configuration.</p>
<p><strong>File encryption is multi-threaded</strong>. The AES 128 CTR or ChaCha20 algorithm can be used too encrypt file contents depending on the settings, with nonce vectors containing 8 or 12 null bytes respectively. In addition, various file encryption modes can be used; below are their brief descriptions.</p>
<div id="attachment_23579" style="width: 959px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23579" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23579" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20949%20419'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Available command line parameters" width="949" height="419" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/15-min-1-1.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23579" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23579" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/15-min-1-1.png" alt="Available command line parameters" width="949" height="419" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-23579" class="wp-caption-text">Available command line parameters</p></div>
<p>&nbsp;</p>
</div>

</tr>
<tr>

</tr>
<tr>
<td width="30%">&#8211;ui</td>
<td width="70%">Launch the ransomware with a graphical interface displaying the encryption progress.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p><strong>BlackCat configuration data</strong> <strong>is contained in the body of the ransomware</strong> in the JSON format. In earlier BlackCat versions, the configuration data was in plain text, while in the latest versions (ALPHV MORPH), it is stored in an encrypted form (AES-128 CTR). For decryption, the first 16 characters of the access token are used as the key. If the characters are entered incorrectly, the ransomware will not be able to run due to a configuration data error.</p>
<div id="attachment_23580" style="width: 860px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23580" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23580" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20850%20436'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Formatted BlackCat configuration data" width="850" height="436" data-lazy-src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/16-min-3.png" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23580" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23580" src="https://website.cdn.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/16-min-3.png" alt="Formatted BlackCat configuration data" width="850" height="436" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-23580" class="wp-caption-text">Formatted BlackCat configuration data</p></div>
</div>

</tr>
<tr>
<td width="30%">default_file_mode (DotPattern, HeadOnly, SmartPattern, AdvancedSmartPattern, Full, Auto)</td>
<td width="70%">Default file encryption mode. DotPattern – encryption with blocks with an interval. HeadOnly – encryption of the initial part. SmartPattern – encryption with blocks with an interval based on percentage of the file size. AdvancedSmartPattern – encryption with blocks with an interval based on the file size with advanced settings. Full – full encryption of file contents. Auto – automatic selection of the file encryption method based on the file type and size.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="30%">default_file_cipher (Best, Aes, ChaCha20)</td>
<td width="70%">Default file encryption algorithm. Best – if there is hardware support for AES (AES-NI), files are encrypted using AES-128 CTR, otherwise, using ChaCha20.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="30%">credentials</td>
<td width="70%">List of stolen credentials of the victim.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="30%">kill_services</td>
<td width="70%">List of services to be stopped.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="30%">kill_processes</td>
<td width="70%">List of substrings with names of processes to be ended.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="30%">exclude_directory_names</td>
<td width="70%">List of directory names excluded during encryption.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="30%">exclude_file_names</td>
<td width="70%">List of file names excluded during encryption.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="30%">exclude_file_extensions</td>
<td width="70%">List of file extensions excluded during encryption.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="30%">exclude_file_path_wildcard</td>
<td width="70%">List of file path wildcards excluded during encryption.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="30%">enable_network_discovery (true, false)</td>
<td width="70%">Encrypts files on available network resources.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="30%">enable_self_propagation (true, false)</td>
<td width="70%">Enables self-propagation in the network (worm mode). The PsExec utility and credentials specified in the value of the parameter &#8220;credentials&#8221; are used for the propagation process.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="30%">enable_set_wallpaper (true, false)</td>
<td width="70%">Sets an image with a message about files being encrypted as a wallpaper.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="30%">enable_esxi_vm_kill (true, false)</td>
<td width="70%">Stops virtual machines.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="30%">enable_esxi_vm_snapshot_kill (true, false)</td>
<td width="70%">Destroys virtual machine snapshots.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="30%">strict_include_paths</td>
<td width="70%">List of paths for encrypting files.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="30%">esxi_vm_kill_exclude</td>
<td width="70%">Whitelist of virtual machine names.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>It must be noted that despite some of the group’s methods being sophisticated, <strong>many tactics, techniques and procedures employed by BlackCat affiliates can be easily detected</strong>, which indicates serious flaws in organizations’ security systems as well as a shortage of skilled security specialists.</p>

                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="promo-block"><h2 class="title-h3 mb-xs-16 mb-sm-16 mb-24">Ransomware Uncovered  2021/2022</h2><p>The well-known complete guide to the latest tactics, techniques, and procedures of ransomware operators based on MITRE ATT&CK®</p><div class="buttons-wrapper mt-md-48 mt-sm-32 mt-xs-24"><a href="https://www.group-ib.com/resources/research-hub/ransomware-2022/" class="button button--filled button--md "><span class="effect slide-up"><span class="effect__label">Download the report</span><span class="effect__label" aria-hidden="true">Download the report</span></span></a></div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><h2>Notable BlackCat Attacks and Affected Industries</h2>
<p>BlackCat/ALPHV targets large enterprises and institutions that use sensitive data or critical services. Below, we’ve highlighted a few high-profile incidents demonstrating the group’s devastating impact and preferred targets.</p>
<h3>Change Healthcare – Healthcare (Feb 2024)</h3>
<p>Hospitals and health providers are seen as high-value targets (with lives and patient care at stake), which ransomware groups tend to exploit. In February 2024, BlackCat infiltrated Change Healthcare, causing system outages in claims processing and pharmacy services nationwide​. BlackCat attackers stole data on 190 million individuals, including insurance member IDs, diagnoses, and Social Security numbers, making it the most significant healthcare breach in U.S. history​​ and BlackCat’s most notorious attack.</p>
<p>A $22 million ransom was paid to prevent the release of the stolen data. However, the BlackCat ransomware group performed an exit scam, pocketed the ransom payment, and didn’t pay the affiliate who conducted the attack. The affiliate then worked with another ransomware group, <a href="/blog/ransomhub-raas/">RansomHub</a>, which attempted to extort Change Healthcare further. No additional ransom payments were made, and the stolen data remains in the hands of cybercriminals.</p>
<p>In a 2023 incident that parallels the attack on Change Healthcare, BlackCat infiltrated a system used for radiation oncology, exfiltrating clinical photographs of breast cancer patients. When Lehigh Valley Health Network (LVHN) refused to pay the ransom, BlackCat <a href="https://www.hipaajournal.com/ransomware-gang-ups-the-ante-by-publishing-naked-images-of-patients/">escalated its tactics by publishing the nude images</a> on its dark web leak site, a new low in ransomware extortion strategies. In response, LVHN agreed to a $65 million class action settlement (one of the largest per-capita settlements in a healthcare data breach case).</p>
<h3>MGM Resorts International – Hospitality/Gambling (Sep 2023)</h3>
<p>In a highly publicized incident, BlackCat (in partnership with the social-engineering group <a href="/blog/ransomware-debris/">Scattered Spider</a>) attacked <a href="https://therecord.media/mgm-resorts-cyberattack-cost-millions">MGM</a>, one of the largest casino resort operators. In a full compromise, BlackCat and Scattered Spider stole customer data, including names, SSNs, driver’s licenses, and passport numbers.</p>
<p>The initial breach was achieved via a phone call to MGM’s IT helpdesk, tricking an employee into revealing credentials​. Once inside, the attackers deployed ransomware that forced MGM to shut down IT systems across all its Las Vegas properties as a containment measure​.</p>
<p>MGM disclosed that the business impact was over $100 million in lost revenue and remediation costs​. Interestingly, MGM refused to pay the ransom, attempting to recover on its own, which likely contributed to the prolonged outage.</p>
<h3>Caesars Entertainment – Hospitality/Gambling (Sep 2023)</h3>
<p>Just days before the MGM hack became public, <a href="https://www.forbes.com/sites/suzannerowankelleher/2023/09/14/2-casino-ransomware-attacks-caesars-mgm/">Caesars Entertainment</a> was hit by a similar attack. Caesars admitted in an SEC filing that hackers (believed to be the same BlackCat/Scattered Spider team) accessed its network via a third-party IT vendor compromise and stole customer data, including many loyalty program member records.</p>
<p>To avoid MGM&#8217;s fate, Caesars paid the attackers $15 million of the demanded $30 million ransom​. The quick payment apparently prevented the encryption of Caesars’ systems—in contrast to MGM, Caesars experienced no major downtime. However, tens of millions of customer records were stolen. This pair of casino attacks raised alarm in corporate boardrooms worldwide about ransomware groups&#8217; potency of social engineering tactics.</p>
<p>Our incident response team, having handled hundreds of ransomware cases, finds it remarkable. “Companies are pouring hundreds of millions of dollars into sophisticated defenses—preventive security, threat detection, endpoint response, you name it. Yet attackers are still breaking in using the simplest methods imaginable: Click this link and enter your credentials. Sometimes the weakest link isn&#8217;t technical &#8211; it&#8217;s human.”</p>
<h3>Reddit – Technology/Social Media (Feb 2023)</h3>
<p>As mentioned earlier in the article, BlackCat claimed responsibility for a breach of <a href="https://www.reddit.com/r/reddit/comments/10y427y/we_had_a_security_incident_heres_what_we_know/">Reddit </a>in 2023. In Reddit’s case, an employee was phished, compromising internal documents and source code.</p>
<p>BlackCat attackers demanded $4.5 million and the rollback of Reddit’s API pricing changes (a rare instance of a quasi-political demand)​. Reddit did not pay, and BlackCat leaked some of the stolen data on its site.</p>
<p>While the platform wasn’t taken down (no ransomware deployed), the Reddit hack serves as a wake-up call for other companies to bolster their cybersecurity measures, train their employees regularly, and consider cybersecurity implications in their business decisions.</p>
<h3>Estée Lauder – Consumer Goods (July 2023)</h3>
<p>BlackCat infiltrated Estée Lauder, a global cosmetics company, in July 2023. In a curious twist, <a href="https://www.computerweekly.com/news/366545044/BlackCat-and-Clop-gangs-both-claim-cyber-attack-on-Estee-Lauder">Estée Lauder was concurrently hit by the Clop ransomware</a> via a supply-chain (MOVEit) vulnerability​. Both BlackCat and Clop claimed responsibility for the breach​. BlackCat’s involvement included accessing the corporate network and exfiltrating data.</p>
<p>Group-IB investigators find the overlap of two major ransomware groups in one incident to be unusual and possibly coincidental. Furthermore, we observe a widening gap between cybersecurity measures and real digital maturity, where an organization can fall victim to multiple actors if multiple vulnerabilities exist.</p>
<h3>Critical Infrastructure and Government</h3>
<p>As further indication that no industry is off-limits, BlackCat/ALPHV ransomware and <a href="https://www.group-ib.com/media-center/press-releases/conti-armada-report/">Conti </a>hackers were said to have been behind the <a href="https://therecord.media/string-of-cyberattacks-on-european-oil-and-chemical-sectors-likely-not-coordinated-officials-say">February 2022 cyberattacks</a> that affected oil transport and storage companies across Europe. At the same time, large-scale cyberattacks have also targeted port facilities in Belgium, Germany, and the Netherlands.</p>
<p>IT systems were disrupted at SEA-Invest in Belgium and Evos in the Netherlands, and there are further reports that BlackCat ransomware has compromised systems at Oiltanking GmbH Group and Mabanaft Group in Germany. This wave of attacks is reminiscent of <a href="https://www.group-ib.com/blog/blackmatter-ransomware/">DarkSide’</a>s ransomware attack on <a href="https://www.nytimes.com/2021/05/08/us/politics/cyberattack-colonial-pipeline.html">Colonial Pipeline</a>, a major U.S. fuel pipeline operator.</p>
<p>In another BlackCat attack, <a href="https://www.financecolombia.com/epm-falls-victim-to-ransomware-attack/">EPM </a>(a Colombian energy supplier) was forced to halt operations after falling victim to the ransomware group. A DOJ 2023 <a href="https://www.justice.gov/archives/opa/pr/justice-department-disrupts-prolific-alphvblackcat-ransomware-variant">press release</a> later reported BlackCat had impacted networks that support U.S. government agencies and critical infrastructure, including sectors like transportation or manufacturing.</p>
<p><b>A pattern emerges:</b> ALPHV’s sting is felt across industries like healthcare, retail, hospitality, technology, and government. While not all incidents come to light, we believe that BlackCat affiliates are prowling for high-value (and lucrative) targets that can’t afford downtime. The geographic reach is also broad, including the U.S., Europe, and Asia. That said, BlackCat and its affiliates are Russian-speaking and avoid attacking CIS (former Soviet) countries by code design, as is common with ransomware from that region.</p>
<p><b>The key takeaway from these incidents </b>is that BlackCat attacks lead to multi-faceted crises: operational disruption, data breach notification, regulatory fines, customer lawsuits, and steep recovery costs. Many victims suffer extortion twice (paying ransom and dealing with data breach fallout).</p>
<h2>BlackCat Ransomware Exit Scam and Rebranding</h2>
<p>BlackCat’s trajectory took a dramatic turn in March 2024 after the massive $22 million ransom payment from Change Healthcare. Shortly after the payoff, BlackCat closed its leak site and announced the sale of its RaaS source code for USD 5 million.</p>
<p>The gang announced that its infrastructure had been compromised by “the feds,” even putting up a seizure message. In reality, this proved to be a ruse. In our <a href="/resources/research-hub/ransomware-readiness/">Ransomware Readiness white paper</a>, Group-IB threat intelligence analysts note that BlackCat pretended the FBI seized its site as cover for an exit scam.</p>
<p>Law enforcement actions around the same time (the U.S. DOJ reported seizing BlackCat servers in late 2023) may have contributed to pressure​, but the catalyst for BlackCat’s shutdown appears to have been greed—the administrators cashing out after their record-breaking ransom success. This betrayal caused BlackCat affiliates to migrate to other ransomware programs to continue their attacks.</p>
<p>A few months after BlackCat’s so-called “goodbye,” a suspiciously familiar ransomware operation surfaced. A new RaaS group calling itself <b>Cicada3301</b> began advertising on darknet forums and listing its first victims.</p>
<p>While some believe this may be a rebrand, it’s difficult to say for sure whether Cicada3301 fully adopted the ALPHV/BlackCat ransomware codebase. It’s more plausible that the group <b>borrowed specific functionalities</b>, such as anti-recovery commands, rather than integrating the full source code.</p>
<p>Though code-level similarities exist, distinct differences point to a partial reuse rather than a full clone. That said, <b>if anyone did get th</b>e<b>ir hands on ALPHV’s source</b>, Cicada3301 appears to be the most likely candidate.</p>
<p>Our researchers observed that Cicada3301’s malware bore strong code-level similarities to BlackCat’s. Both were written in Rust, targeted Windows/Linux/ESXi, and even shared particular functionality (e.g., using the same methods to halt VMs and clear logs)​.</p>
<p>Discover how Group-IB’s threat intelligence analysts <a href="/blog/cicada3301/">infiltrated the Cicada3301 Ransomware group</a> in 2024.</p>
<p><b>Key observations:</b> Compared to BlackCat’s brazen attacks, Cicada3301 targets mostly smaller businesses via common attack vectors like exposed RDP (using stolen or weak credentials)​. Given the heat on BlackCat, this could be an attempt to stay under law enforcement’s radar.</p>
<p>According to Group-IB’s <a href="/landing/high-tech-crime-trends-2025/">High-Tech Crime Trends 2025 report</a>, BlackCat’s exit scam (along with a similar scam by the NoEscape gang) undermined confidence among ransomware affiliates. Our analysts believe this could lead to affiliates being quicker to jump ship or demanding more decentralized control to avoid being left empty-handed.</p>
<p>We may also see new groups and extortion models rise to fill the vacuum. Indeed, within months of BlackCat’s disappearance, multiple new RaaS brands (like Cicada3301 and others) were already on the scene. Organizations today must be vigilant as threat actors shift identities and tactics in response to mounting legal pressure.</p>
<h2>Defensive Strategies Against BlackCat Ransomware</h2>
<p>Below are key defensive recommendations and best practices informed by recent ransomware incidents and Group-IB’s frontline cyber threat investigations:<b></b></p>
<h3>1. Strengthen Authentication and Access Controls</h3>
<p>Since BlackCat often gained entry via stolen or weak credentials, organizations should enforce multi-factor authentication (MFA) on all remote access and sensitive accounts.</p>
<ul>
<li aria-level="1">Use phishing-resistant MFA like hardware security keys (FIDO2/WebAuthn) or certificate-based authentication. Traditional OTP apps or push MFA can be compromised by SIM swapping or push fatigue tactics that BlackCat affiliates use​.</li>
<li aria-level="1">Ensure that MFA is enabled for VPNs, RDP gateways, email, and administrative accounts.</li>
<li aria-level="1">Passwords should never be reused across services. Implementing password managers and regularly rotating privileged credentials can help reduce the risk of credential dumps.</li>
<li aria-level="1">Practice the principle of least privilege through an <a href="https://www.group-ib.com/resources/knowledge-hub/identity-and-access-management/">identity and access management system (IAM)</a>. Privileged access management (PAM) solutions create additional authentication barriers for admin access and isolate privileged sessions, making it harder for attackers to leap from an employee account to a domain admin account.</li>
</ul>
<h3>2. Improve User Awareness</h3>
<p>Group-IB has identified several “persuasive” emails and text messages used by BlackCat with legitimate-sounding details (e.g., package delivery notices) to lure users​. Given these cunning social engineering tactics, we recommend <b>prioritizing user awareness training</b> as your cybersecurity defense&#8217;s critical, human-centered layer.</p>
<ul>
<li aria-level="1">Conduct regular security awareness training covering phishing, spear-phishing, and phone-based scams.</li>
<li aria-level="1">Teach staff how to verify unsolicited contacts claiming to be IT support, and to be wary of any message urging urgent action on their account.</li>
<li aria-level="1">Simulate monthly phishing attacks to test employees – this can identify who might need extra training.</li>
<li aria-level="1">Ensure there’s a 24/7 open channel for employees to report suspected phishing or strange IT requests.</li>
</ul>
<h3>3. Close Common Entry Points</h3>
<p>BlackCat actively targets known vulnerabilities in external-facing systems, making <a href="/resources/knowledge-hub/patch-management/">patch management</a> a key defensive strategy.</p>
<ul>
<li aria-level="1">Prioritize patching any vulnerabilities known to be exploited in the wild (<a href="https://www.cisa.gov/known-exploited-vulnerabilities-catalog">CISA’s database of exploited vulnerabilities</a> is a good guide).</li>
<li aria-level="1">Mitigate Exchange bugs like ProxyShell, update remote desktop services, and apply fixes for any widely used software (VPNs, virtualization, etc.).</li>
<li aria-level="1">If a system cannot be patched immediately, consider taking it offline or applying interim mitigation (like disabling an affected feature or adding WAF rules).</li>
<li aria-level="1"><a href="/products/attack-surface-management/">Attack surface management</a> solutions help prioritize remediation tasks with threat intelligence insights. Security teams can continuously scan the entire IPv4 space and beyond to identify all Internet-facing assets, including shadow IT, forgotten infrastructure, and misconfigurations that may expose an internal asset to the open web.</li>
<li aria-level="1">Use network segmentation to separate critical servers (databases, domain controllers, and backups) from the user network.</li>
</ul>
<h3>4. Detect and Respond to Intrusions Quickly</h3>
<p>Deploy advanced <a href="/resources/knowledge-hub/edr-endpoint-detection-and-response/">Endpoint Detection and Response (EDR)</a> tools on servers and workstations.</p>
<ul>
<li aria-level="1">EDR can catch suspicious behavior, such as credential dumping, unusual PowerShell execution, or a ransomware binary trying to mass-encrypt files. Advanced EDR solutions can even halt encryption in progress.</li>
<li aria-level="1">Ensure that logging is enabled and centralized. Windows Event Logs (especially Security logs, Sysmon logs), firewall logs, VPN access logs, and DNS logs should feed into a <a href="/resources/knowledge-hub/security-information-and-event-management/">SIEM </a>or monitoring system where alerts can be generated.</li>
<li aria-level="1">Use <a href="/blog/fraud-protection-know-your-real-users/">fraud protection behavioral analytics</a> to flag anomalies, such as an account logging in at odd hours, an admin tool running on a non-admin machine, or a workstation suddenly initiating connections to dozens of other PCs or to an IP in a foreign country.</li>
<li aria-level="1">Conduct regular incident response drills (tabletop exercises and live simulations) for a ransomware scenario. The speed of BlackCat’s encryption (often minutes to an hour for an enterprise) means every second counts once an attack is detected. Ensure your team knows how to isolate an infected machine quickly, disconnect from the VPN or domain to stop propagation, and activate backups.</li>
<li aria-level="1">Group-IB recommends exercising your security program against behaviors mapped to frameworks like MITRE ATT&amp;CK to validate that your controls can detect or prevent those techniques.</li>
</ul>
<h3>5. Protect and Isolate Backups</h3>
<p>Follow the 3-2-1 backup rule by keeping at least three copies of critical data, on two different media, with one copy offline and offsite.</p>
<ul>
<li aria-level="1">Ransomware cannot access offline (immutable) backups. You can use disk or cloud backup services that offer immutability (write-once-read-many storage).</li>
<li aria-level="1">Ensure backup admin interfaces are not exposed to the general network and that MFA is required to access them. Segment backup systems from the domain if possible (so domain admin creds alone can’t delete backups).</li>
<li aria-level="1">Implement delayed deletion for cloud backups (so that if an attacker tries to delete them, they remain recoverable for a period).</li>
<li aria-level="1">Secure backup credentials. BlackCat ransomware analysis shows that attackers have tools to extract backup software passwords​, so use strong, unique credentials for backup systems and monitor login attempts to those consoles.</li>
<li aria-level="1">If using Windows Shadow Copies, you can monitor or lock the VSS admin functions to prevent unauthorized deletion. In the event of an incident, having intact backups that ransomware groups couldn’t encrypt will reduce the temptation to pay a ransom.</li>
</ul>
<h3>6. Incident Response and External Support</h3>
<p>If, despite all efforts, your organization ends up in an attacker’s crosshairs, ensure you have a strong Incident Response (IR) plan ready.</p>
<ul>
<li aria-level="1">Your incident response plan must clearly define steps for technical containment (like network isolation and immediate password resets), internal and external communications, legal protocols, and interactions with law enforcement.</li>
<li aria-level="1">Maintain an <a href="/services/incident-response-retainer/">incident response retainer</a> with cybersecurity firms where dedicated IR specialists can assist in comprehensive analysis, containment, and remediation to restore operations fast.</li>
</ul>
<p>For an accurate assessment of your organization’s current readiness, Group-IB’s <a href="/resources/research-hub/cybersecurity-ultimate-assessment-guide/">Cybersecurity Ultimate Assessment Guide</a> helps you evaluate your security posture, uncover vulnerabilities, and take decisive actions to strengthen defenses against emerging threats.</p>
<h2>How Group-IB Helps Organizations Stay Ahead of Ransomware</h2>
<p>As ransomware groups continue to innovate, Group-IB has helped organizations create a resilient and secure environment through strategic threat intelligence and expert incident response.</p>
<p>Here’s how our approach works to defend against and outrun threats like BlackCat:</p>
<ol>
<li aria-level="1"><b>Early warning:</b> Group-IB <a href="/products/threat-intelligence/">Threat Intelligence Platform</a> (with over 850 threat actor profiles) helps you to understand ransomware trends and anticipate attacks by monitoring dark web forums, leak sites, and malware developments. You’ll receive actionable insights into attacker behaviors to strengthen your defenses against emerging ransomware tactics.</li>
<li aria-level="1"><b>24/7 threat monitoring and detection: </b>For real-time defense, our <a href="/products/managed-xdr/">Managed Extended Detection and Response (XDR)</a> is built with threat hunting and intel to monitor your endpoints, network, and cloud. We can detect early signs of ransomware, like unusual admin activities, and isolate affected systems, evicting adversaries before they detonate ransomware. To guarantee peace of mind, an expert <a href="/services/compromise-assessment/">Compromise Assessment</a> can identify hidden threats and cybersecurity gaps that could lead to incidents in the future.</li>
<li aria-level="1"><b>Reduce potential attack surface:</b> Our external <a href="/products/attack-surface-management/">Attack Surface Management</a> solution scans and assesses external-facing assets for weaknesses. It will highlight exposed RDP ports, out-of-date VPN appliances, or forgotten websites that could serve as entry points​.</li>
<li aria-level="1"><b>Secure email gateways:</b> Many ransomware attacks start with a phishing email. You can block phishing attempts before they reach employees with <a href="/products/business-email-protection/">Business Email Protection</a>, which recognizes phishing domains or malware attachments.</li>
<li aria-level="1"><b>Restore business continuity: </b>In the event of an attack, the Group-IB <a href="/services/incident-response/">Incident Response</a> team can jump in to identify the intrusion vector, secure the network, and help restore systems. Our goal is to minimize downtime and data loss. This includes negotiating with threat actors, coordinating with law enforcement, and leveraging available decryption tools or keys.</li>
</ol>
<p>No single tool or strategy can stop a sophisticated RaaS group like BlackCat, but a layered defense can raise the cost and difficulty of attack to the point where the adversary might turn tail, or move on to an easier target.</p>
<p>Recognized by <a href="https://www.group-ib.com/media-center/press-releases/gartner-market-guide-for-security-threat-intelligence/">Gartner </a>as a Representative Vendor in the Market Guide for Security Threat Intelligence Products and Services, Group-IB offers the industry’s most complete and detailed insights into threat actors and their activities. We continuously update our tools and methodologies to integrate new detection analytics for malware variants, or tracking the emergence of BlackCat rebrands like Cicada3301.</p>
<p>With the latest intelligence, robust security controls, and expert response capabilities, businesses can defend themselves and significantly reduce the risk of falling victim. Explore Group-IB’s <a href="/solutions/ransomware-protection/">solutions for ransomware protection</a> to give your organization a decisive advantage.</p>
<h2>FAQs</h2>
<h3>What is BlackCat ransomware?</h3>
<p>BlackCat ransomware (also known as ALPHV) is a sophisticated Ransomware-as-a-Service (RaaS) operation that targets enterprises with double extortion tactics by encrypting data and threatening to leak it unless a ransom is paid.</p>
<h3>How does BlackCat ransomware spread?</h3>
<p>BlackCat spreads through stolen credentials, phishing emails, VPN exploits, and Remote Desktop Protocol (RDP) attacks. It often uses social engineering or known vulnerabilities to gain initial access.</p>
<h3>What industries are most targeted by BlackCat?</h3>
<p>BlackCat ransomware attacks target industries where downtime or data breaches cause significant harm or financial loss, especially healthcare, hospitality, technology, energy, and critical infrastructure.</p>
<h3>What encryption techniques does BlackCat use?</h3>
<p>BlackCat uses strong AES and RSA encryption methods, customizing its payload to maximize impact across Windows, Linux, and ESXi systems during a ransomware attack.</p>
<h3>How does BlackCat evade detection and security measures?</h3>
<p>To evade threat detection, BlackCat disables security tools, clears logs, abuses legitimate admin tools, and operates stealthily using customized scripts and malware built in Rust.</p>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    const postcontent = document.getElementById('blog-post-content');
    const h2counter = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2');

    for (i = 0; i < h2counter.length; i++) {
        const h2elementtag = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2')[i];
        const h2element = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2')[i].innerHTML;
        let postnavitem = document.createElement('a');
        postnavitem.classList.add('post-navigation__item', 'subtitle2', 'scrollto');
        postnavitem.innerHTML = h2element;
        const postnavlink = postnavitem.innerHTML;
        let str = postnavlink;
        str = str.replace(/<[^>]*>/g, '');
        str = str.replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9\s\u0400-\u04FF]/g, '');
        str = str.replace(/\s+/g, '-').toLowerCase();
        str = str.replace(/^-+|-+$/g, '');
        postnavitem.classList += ' ' + str;
        postnavitem.href = '#' + str;
        h2elementtag.id = str;
        document.getElementById('post-navigation__wrapper').appendChild(postnavitem);
    }

    const sections = document.querySelectorAll('h2');
    const navItems = document.querySelectorAll('.post-navigation__item');

    window.addEventListener('scroll', () => {
        let current = '';
        sections.forEach((section) => {
            const sectionTop = section.offsetTop;
            const sectionHeight = section.clientHeight;
            if (pageYOffset >= sectionTop - sectionHeight / 10) {
                current = section.getAttribute('id');
            }
        });

        navItems.forEach((a) => {
            a.classList.remove('active');
            if (a.classList.contains(current)) {
                a.classList.add('active');
            }
        });
    });

    // SMOOTH SCROLL
    var $root = jQuery('html, body');


    jQuery('a[href^="#"].scrollto').click(function() {
        var href = jQuery.attr(this, 'href');
        var headerHeight = 100;

        $root.animate({
                scrollTop: jQuery(href).offset().top - headerHeight
            },
            300,
        );

        return false;
    });

    // SMOOTH SCROLL WITH NO HASH
    var $root = jQuery('html, body');


    jQuery('a[href^="#"].scrolltonohash').click(function() {
        var href = jQuery.attr(this, 'href');
        var headerHeight = 100;

        $root.animate({
                scrollTop: jQuery(href).offset().top - headerHeight
            },
            300
        );

        return false;
    });
</script>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    function readingTime() {
        const text = document.getElementById("blog-post-content").innerText;
        const wpm = 225;
        const words = text.trim().split(/\s+/).length;
        const time = Math.ceil(words / wpm);
        document.getElementById("reading-time").innerText = time;
    }
    readingTime();
</script>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    document.getElementById("share-url").value = window.location.href;
    document.getElementById("share-url-mobile").value = window.location.href;
    jQuery('.share-url__copy').click(function() {
        jQuery('.share-url__copy').addClass('success');
        navigator.clipboard.writeText(window.location.href);
        setTimeout(() => {
            jQuery('.share-url__copy').removeClass('success');
        }, 1500);
    });
</script>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    var carousel123 = new Swiper('.carousel123', {
        grabCursor: true,
        pagination: {
            el: '.swiper-pagination',
            clickable: true,
        },
    });

    var authorsMoreThan2Swiper = new Swiper('.authors-more-than-2-swiper', {
        grabCursor: true,
        pagination: {
            el: '.swiper-pagination',
            clickable: true,
        },
        breakpoints: {
            0: {
                slidesPerView: 'auto',
                spaceBetween: 16,
                slidesPerView: 1,
                slidesPerGroup: 1,
            },
            620: {
                spaceBetween: 24,
                slidesPerView: 2,

    function positioningSidebar() {
        let sidebar = document.getElementById('sidebar');
        let sidebarHeight = sidebar.offsetHeight;
        let windowHeight = document.documentElement.clientHeight;
        if (windowHeight < (sidebarHeight + 48)) {
            let sidebarAuthors = sidebar.querySelector('.sidebar__authors');
            let sidebarMorePosts = sidebar.querySelector('.sidebar__more-posts');
            let authorsHeight = sidebarAuthors ? sidebarAuthors.offsetHeight : 0;
            let morePostsHeight = sidebarMorePosts ? sidebarMorePosts.offsetHeight : 0;
            let sidebarTop = authorsHeight + morePostsHeight + 140;
            jQuery(sidebar).css('top', -sidebarTop + 'px');
        } else {
            jQuery(sidebar).css('top', '72px');
        }
    }
    window.addEventListener('resize', positioningSidebar);
    positioningSidebar();

    if (localStorage.getItem('theme')) {
        if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'dark') {
            jQuery('body').addClass('theme--dark');
        } else if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'light') {
            jQuery('body').addClass('theme--white');
        }
    } else {
        localStorage.setItem('theme', 'dark');
        jQuery('body').addClass('theme--dark');
    }

    jQuery('.theme-toggle').click(function() {
        if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'dark') {
            localStorage.setItem('theme', 'light');
        } else {
            localStorage.setItem('theme', 'dark');
        }
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('no-transition');
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('theme--dark');
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('theme--white');
        setTimeout(() => {
            jQuery('body').toggleClass('no-transition');
        }, 250);
    });
    jQuery('.share-toggle').click(function() {
        jQuery('.share-toggle').toggleClass('active');
        jQuery('.share-widget').toggleClass('show');
    });
</script>
